Every still ships a `.pane.txt` ground-truth dump, an anchor-verified PASS/FAIL table, and near-blank variance detection; typing uses an Esc-before-Enter protocol to close the debounced autocomplete popup; `AddonsJsonGuard` restores the seeded addons.json byte-for-byte; the todo script crafts a session file carrying `unown-todo-state` entries so the overlay mounts without a model turn; the advisor script earns real notes (a plaintext credential committed into git history → a `[concern]` steer card, VLM-verified through the bridge) rather than waiting on benign-task silence. 11/11 addon surfaces captured and verified live, including the ask_user_question dialog end-to-end through the Agnes failover while Dashscope was quota-throttled; README.md and docs/tui-capture-workflow.md carry the lane's contract.


### Agent Behavior (web results store — stale-while-revalidate, 2026-10-20)

- **webcache: stale-while-revalidate — past-TTL answers serve instantly (marked) while a background refresh restores freshness (RFC 5861)** — until now a stale-but-windowed entry blocked the caller on a fresh provider round (or, if every attempt failed, fell back to the flagged stale answer). The store now implements the other half of RFC 5861: a `web_search` or validator-less `webfetch` re-ask past TTL and inside the window answers **immediately** from the store, marked `· stale — refreshing in background (61m old)` so the model knows exactly what it is reading, while a detached background refresh — collapsed through its own single-flight key, never throwing into the serve path, bounded by `UNOWN_WEB_CACHE_SWR_MS` (default: the entry's own stale-if-error window) — restores freshness so the *next* identical call is a plain hit. Production precedent: nginx's `proxy_cache_use_stale updating` and Varnish grace mode. Measured on the bench receipts: a stale re-ask that would have paid a 101 ms provider round serves in **0.88 ms** (115×), the background refresh is exactly one upstream call (asserted), and the follow-up call is a fresh hit (asserted). Fetches **with** validators keep their exact blocking 304 revalidation path and its stale-if-error fall-through unchanged — one cheap conditional GET is worth blocking on when the answer is byte-identical; the raw `webfetch` mode keeps exact semantics deliberately (power users asking for bytes). `UNOWN_WEB_CACHE_SWR=0` restores the fully blocking behavior (the stale-if-error tests pin that mode). A new `swr` bench scenario asserts the whole contract end to end. Pinned by 3 new integration tests in `test/websearch.test.ts` (search SWR serve + exactly-one-background-refresh + next-call-fresh; SWR=0 blocking control; the validator-less fetch twin) with the two stale-if-error tests now explicitly pinning `UNOWN_WEB_CACHE_SWR=0`, plus 5 `eligibleForSwr` policy tests in `test/webcache.test.ts` (fresh/stale/window/knob/bound).

### Added

- **webcache: the performance layer — an in-memory index, a hot-entry memo, RAM recency, and cacache-style writes, measured before/after with committed receipts** — the store was benchmarked end to end first (`scripts/webcache-bench.mjs` — a new seven-scenario harness: per-op latency anatomy across S/M/L payload classes × 200/2k/20k stores, O(N) scan costs, e2e cold/warm/304 through the real call paths against local mock providers, thundering-herd coalescing measured by origin-call counting (the coordinated-omission-safe metric), zipf s=1.0 workload realism, cross-process visibility, memory bounds; percentiles with warmup discard, warm-page-cache disclosure, assertions inside the receipt gating the exit code). The baseline receipt exposed the costs; the fixes follow named prior art, each measured before/after on the same box: **(1) an in-memory index** (`{key → bytes, lastUsed}`, the Chromium SimpleCache model) so `webCacheStats` and eviction stop rescanning the disk — the dashboard polls stats every 8 s and each poll was an event-loop-blocking full scan (**49.9 ms p50 at 20k entries → 0.003 ms** between rebuilds; the 30-call poll-loop over 5k entries went 311.9 → 12.9 ms, 12.74 ms of which is the one rebuild). Counts between rebuilds are cross-process approximations and are **labeled** as such (`stats.index {mode, ageMs, approximate}`; CLI prints "approx", the dashboard tab an "≈ indexed" chip) — the WebKit `m_approximateSize` trade, with eviction correctness never depending on index completeness (the cap self-heals at the next rebuild; `UNOWN_WEB_CACHE_INDEX_MS=0` is the exact always-scan mode). **(2) a hot-entry memo** (cacache's own memoization shape — 500 entries / 50 MiB / 3 min, `UNOWN_WEB_CACHE_MEMO*` knobs) so repeat hits skip the disk read, JSON parse and sha256 re-verify entirely while honoring their own entry TTL: a 48 KB page hit went **0.211 → 0.001 ms (~200×)**, the 192 KB cap class ~1,500×, e2e warm search 0.029 → 0.013 ms (10,225× vs cold). **(3) RAM recency** replaces the per-hit `utimes` metadata write (Chromium's `UseIfExists` rule; the same write-amplification Linux built `lazytime`/`relatime` to avoid, unreadable on NTFS/FAT anyway) and sweeps iterate the index — the sweep at 20k went **47.9 ms → 0.002 ms p50 (0.16 ms max over 200 consecutive sweeps)**. **(4) cache writes are tmp+rename without fsync** — the cacache write model; a cache entry is re-fetchable, so process-crash safety is the right durability point and a power-loss-torn entry is caught by the payload checksum and re-fetched (LevelDB's db_bench: fsync-per-write ≈ 150× a buffered write; npm's own history carries 3–8× install regressions from fsync-heavy phases on Docker/network mounts). `UNOWN_WEB_CACHE_DURABLE=1` restores the double-fsync; the memory tiers keep theirs — a lost learning note is gone forever, a lost cache entry is a refetch. **(5) stats reads a 128 KiB tail** of the events feed instead of the whole file. An honest correction shipped with it: the baseline RESULTS table had blamed the amortized-put p99 on the eviction sweep, but the after-run showed the same tail while 200 consecutive sweeps measured 0.002 ms — the tail was CPU contention on the 2-core test box, and `benchmarks/webcache/RESULTS.md` records the correction because both receipts' raw samples make it checkable. Receipts: `benchmarks/webcache/results/webcache-bench-1792496271356-baseline.json` (10/10 assertions) and `…-after.json` (17/17), methodology + track README under `benchmarks/webcache/`. Docs: the design record's new performance-layer section (`docs/web-results-store.md`), the knob table (`docs/configuration.md` — `UNOWN_WEB_CACHE_INDEX_MS`, `_MEMO*`, `_DURABLE`, `_SWR*`), usage + BEHAVIOR store contracts, and the CLI/dashboard approx labels. Pinned by 17 new tests in `test/webcache.test.ts` (index modes + put-tracking + clear-resets-index + index-driven sweep + memo root-scoping/corruption-bypass/TTL/clock/cap + SWR policy + durability round-trips + the events tail read) with a file-level `resetWebCacheInternals()` isolation hook; full suites green: self-learning 2504 pass / 0 fail, dashboard tab 8/8.

- **bench: the webcache benchmark track — `scripts/webcache-bench.mjs` + receipts** — see the perf-layer entry above; the harness is a first-class repo track (`benchmarks/webcache/`, indexed in the benchmarks README's testbench table), runnable as `bun scripts/webcache-bench.mjs [--quick|--only <scenarios>|--label <suffix>]`, with receipts carrying every raw latency sample (no hand-copied numbers anywhere), environment honesty (runtime, cpus, commit, warm-page-cache note), and exit-code-gating assertions so CI can run it as a correctness+perf check in one pass.

### Fixed

- **doctor: the AGENTS.md staleness audit now warns only on confident staleness — 31 false positives → 0 on this repo's own AGENTS.md** — the audit flagged any backtick-quoted token containing a slash or a known extension that did not resolve from the project root, but real AGENTS.md prose legitimately backticks package-relative shorthand (`src/cli.ts` meaning `<pkg>/src/cli.ts`), git refs (`origin/main`), bare filenames (`changelog-data.ts`), URL paths (`/api/overview`) and absolute paths (`/usr/local/bin/bun`) — none of which resolve from the root and none of which are staleness. Measured on this repo's own root AGENTS.md: **31 warns, 0 true positives**, which buried the signal the check exists to carry (nobody reads a warn list that is always 31 long). Backtick tokens — prose, a weaker signal than a link — now apply a confidence ladder: the token must be relative, contain a separator, and its **anchor** (first path segment) must exist in the project; anchored-but-missing tails still warn, because an author whose anchor resolves demonstrably knew the tree and the tail is real staleness. Markdown links keep the strict check in both directions (a link is an explicit navigation affordance, not prose emphasis); absolute/URL paths are skipped everywhere — they were never project files. Where the repo's own prose WAS confidently stale shorthand it was corrected at the source: five `src/*.ts` refs in the `bin/unown` paragraph now carry their full `pi-repo/packages/self-learning/src/…` paths (matching the paragraph's own `boot.ts` style), and the end-to-end sanity line reads provider `zai-bridge` with default model `kimi-k2.5` (no slash label). Pinned by the reworked stale-reference tests plus two new ones in `test/doctor-instructions.test.ts` (anchored-missing flags; unanchored / bare / absolute / git-ref prose never flags; links stay strict with absolute link paths skipped) — `bun bin/unown doctor-instructions` on this repo: 0 warnings.
- **scripts: `test_bridge.py` repointed at the bridge it actually tests** — the probe was written when the bridge itself listened on 3032; since the port split that port belongs to the zai-upstream shim, so the probe has been aimed at the wrong service's contract. It now targets 3031 by default (override with `BRIDGE_PORT`) and sends the `BRIDGE_API_KEY` bearer when set, matching the bridge's `authRequired` semantics. `.env.example` also gains the previously-omitted `ZAI_UPSTREAM_PORT` / `ZAI_UPSTREAM_MODEL` knobs (documented as shim-only: they are inert unless `BRIDGE_UPSTREAM_URL` points at the loopback gateway).
- **docs: the dashboard suite's warning noise is now a documented non-issue, not an unchased mystery** — `bun test src` prints ~17 identical `not wrapped in act(...)` warnings (radix `Presence`/`ForwardRef` animation ticks) plus ~26 recharts `width(0) height(0)` warnings per run, all happy-dom artifacts. Measured benign across **27/27 consecutive green suite runs** on the 2-core box (bridge ×6 incl. failover stress, self-learning ×3, integration ×3, dashboard ×2), byte-identical counts run-over-run. `docs/testing-strategy.md` §7.1 now records the signature, the investigation (a preload-level act-flush `afterEach` and an in-file 30 ms flush before `cleanup()` both left the count unchanged — the updates fire mid-test through chained rAF channels, after the last awaited assertion, where an afterEach-scope flush cannot reach), and the revisit condition, so future sessions do not re-chase it. The flake-hunt also re-verified the slow e2e tests already carry explicit generous timeouts (60–180 s) per the repo's own convention — no change needed there.

### Agent Behavior (Interactive Fleet, part 2 — 2026-10-20)

- **fleet: the interactive subagent fleet — async spawn, mid-run steering, ask_question, and resume; the pi-interactive-subagents port on a cross-platform native substrate** — reverse-engineered from amosblomqvist/pi-interactive-subagents and rebuilt WITHOUT tmux on plain subprocesses + atomic control files (identical on Windows / macOS / Linux). The `task` tool gains `async: true` + `name`: fire-and-forget spawn with an immediate ack; the result arrives later as a steer message that triggers a new turn. A new **`task_control`** tool (the cciotti-style control-verb facade) steers a running child mid-task (delivered through pi's native steer queue — absorbed into the current turn or picked up at the next boundary), stops it, reports live status, or lists the fleet; steering a FINISHED child by name **resumes** it — the original session file plus the loadout sidecar (tools/model/effort/MCP/system-prompt/cwd), and a missing loadout is refused, never relaunched unrestricted. **`ask_question`** lets a child ask the orchestrator ONE clarifying question mid-task: the ask file lands as a steered-in message, the child parks inside the tool call until `task_control steer` answers it (a steer while parked IS the answer — live-measured deadlock fix), and a 10-minute timeout degrades to best-judgment guidance. The **live widget** above the editor (theme-aware — the upstream's hardcoded RGB colors were deliberately not ported) shows elapsed / current tool / waiting-for-answer / stalled per child, plus a footer status line and a `/tasks` command; a 1s watcher drives a 120s stall watchdog. **Completion batching** (pi-cciotti port): successes coalesce (150ms debounce / 1s max wait) so an N-child fan-out wakes the parent ONCE; failures and blocked replies bypass the batch. The **resume registry** (`.unown/fleet/registry.json`, 32-entry bound, gitignored) persists name → session across sessions; a new `--session <path>` CLI flag (`SessionManager.open`) is the substrate. Headless `-p` runs **drain**: a NUL-prefixed sentinel message rides as the final prompt, the fleet's `input` handler intercepts it (`{action: "handled"}` — never model-visible) and waits for the fleet so async results land while the process is alive. Worktree isolation composes (trees adopted, disposed on finalize). Two live-measured bugs fixed + regression-pinned: the parked-ask deadlock above, and the watcher re-delivering the standing question on every status flip (steer → active → re-ask → loop). New modules: `fleet.ts` (pure protocols: status machine, control inbox, names, loadout, registry, message builders, batching), `fleet-runtime.ts` (manager + TUI surface), `fleet-child.ts` (child-side extension, injected via cli.ts when `UNOWN_FLEET_CONTROL` is set), `context-brief.ts` (below). Pinned by `test/fleet.test.ts` (21), `test/fleet-runtime.test.ts` (16, fake-spawn harness driving real JSONL event parsing + steer/stop/ask/resume/batching), `test/fleet-child.test.ts` (8), `test/context-brief.test.ts` (9), + `--session` parse-args tests. Verified live end-to-end through the Dashscope stack: async ack → WAITING → child result steered back → new turn; ask_question → task_control steer answer → child wrote the answer to disk → result delivered → registry done.

- **subagents: context briefs — summary mode (pi-cciotti port)** — agents with `context: "summary"` frontmatter spawn with a distilled brief of the parent session prepended to their task (`Context brief from the parent session:\n<brief>\n\nTask:\n<task>`): one cheap LLM call over a newest-first projection (user text + assistant text + tool-call NAMES only, ~24k-char source budget) into a ≤4,096-char role-directed brief (per-agent `contextBrief` shapes what it keeps). Graceful degradation: distillation failures fall back to the fresh context with a visible warning, never abort; short sessions (<80 chars of source) skip the paid call; both lanes (sync task + async fleet) compose it. Live-verified: a summary agent correctly reported the feature name + file the parent had mentioned — knowledge that existed only in the parent session, delivered via the brief with zero tool calls. Pinned by `test/context-brief.test.ts` (9: projection order + tool-name-only + budget cut on the oldest line, prompt shape with/without role instruction, paid-call skip, cap, failure-to-null, delivery framing).

### Fixed

- **fleet: restricted agents regain `ask_question`** — pi's `--tools` allowlist filters extension tools too, so an async child spawned from any agent with a `tools:` frontmatter list (`explore`, `scout`, `reviewer` — every read-only built-in) silently lost the ask channel and could only reply `BLOCKED: I cannot ask questions` (measured live: an `explore` child finished in 4 s with zero tool calls, refusing the task). The fleet spawn path now appends `ask_question` to the allowlist it passes — the ask channel is fleet infrastructure, not agent surface, and the tool still degrades gracefully when no channel exists. Covers first spawn and resume (both build args through the same path). Pinned by a new argv regression test in `test/fleet-runtime.test.ts` (restricted list carries `ask_question` + the agent's own tools; unrestricted spawns gain no `--tools` flag).

### Agent Behavior (Interactive Fleet, part 1 — 2026-10-20)

- **subagents: the advertised catalog, delegation levels, role builtins, the BLOCKED protocol, the completion guard, and fallback models — the pi-cciotti port** — the parent model now *sees* the agent roster instead of guessing it: every session that is not itself a subagent gets a byte-budgeted `<advertised_subagents>` block appended to its system prompt on `before_agent_start` (every discovered agent's name + description, minus `advertise: false` opt-outs, plus the one-sentence delegation policy; 16 agents / 12,288 bytes / 512-byte descriptions hard caps, whole-agent front-dropping with `<omitted count="N"/>` on overflow — built-ins drop first so project agents survive). The policy dial is `UNOWN_DELEGATION_LEVEL` (`never|rarely|standard|aggressive`, default `standard`); `never` withdraws the catalog entirely while the `task` tool keeps working when invoked explicitly. Four role-specialized builtins join `general`/`explore`/`background-explore`: **scout** (read-only recon tuned to come back SMALL — cheap routing, compressed inventory + anchors handoff), **worker** (implementation with the deterministic completion guard ON by default and a fixed Changed/Commands/Residual-risks evidence report), **reviewer** (read-only P0/P1/P2 findings + an explicit BLOCK/OK/OK-with-notes merge verdict, P0 forces BLOCK), and **planner** (writes `plan.md` and stops — the file is the review gate; `advertise: false` so it is reached explicitly, not by browsing). Two fail-closed child contracts port verbatim-in-spirit: the **BLOCKED protocol** (every spawned subagent's system prompt gains boundary instructions ending in `BLOCKED: <short reason>` — a machine-readable refusal lane the parent parses tolerantly (fenced/padded/first-line, never a mid-document mention) and routes as a first-class outcome, never an improvisation) and the **completion guard** (`completionGuard: true` agents are wrapped in a before/after git fingerprint — `git status --porcelain -z` path list + SHA-256 over `git diff HEAD`, both with `core.fsmonitor=false`; identical snapshots + exit 0 + not BLOCKED ⇒ the run reports FAILED with "Subagent completed without making edits for an implementation task."; non-git or git-failure reports `unavailable`, never a false failure; worktree-isolated runs guard inside their worktree). **Fallback models** (frontmatter `fallbackModels`, ordered chain after the agent's `model`): a candidate advances only on a provider-style failure (auth/quota/rate/5xx/timeout/connection reset/silent non-zero exit, regex-classified) AND only when the child had done no work yet (zero tool calls — rotating mid-task would discard real work); hard-failed models get 30-minute health marks in the shared `~/.unown/agent/model-health.json` (atomic Windows-safe writes, fail-closed to try-the-model, TTL-pruned on load) so a whole fleet pays the outage once per window; the report lists the attempted chain and bills usage across it. New pure modules `subagent-catalog.ts` (catalog render + frontmatter parse + BLOCKED parse + guard snapshots; no pi imports) and `model-health.ts` (store + classification), role definitions in `subagent-builtins.ts`, wired through `tools.ts` (task tool + `before_agent_start`). Pinned by `test/subagent-catalog.test.ts` (19: level parsing + sentences, frontmatter plucking incl. snake_case, catalog rendering + count cap + byte budget + UTF-8-safe description capping, BLOCKED shapes + mid-document negative + reason cap, guard snapshots against a real temp git repo incl. tracked-edit + non-git-null paths), `test/model-health.test.ts` (11: round-trip, prune/TTL, corrupt-store fail-closed, unwritable-home never-throws, lead-trim keeps one candidate, provider-failure classification incl. the no-rotate cases), `test/subagent-builtins.test.ts` (6: roster shape, worker guard+chain, scout routing, planner unadvertised, role output contracts), and 4 new wiring tests in `test/tools.test.ts` (catalog injection into `before_agent_start` system prompts, no-event no-op, `never` withdrawal, subagent suppression). Engine suite: 2210 pass / 0 fail / 38 skip across 80 files. Standing invariant preserved: no fork of the pi substrate; the catalog lands on the documented `before_agent_start` systemPrompt seam, everything else extends `SubagentConfig` + the task tool.

### Agent Behavior (TUI addon framework — 2026-10-20)

- **addons: the TUI addon framework — `ask_user_question`, the addon-era `todo`, and the `advisor`, all user-configurable via `~/.unown/agent/addons.json` + `/addons`** — the session surface gains three addons bundled in one `unown-addons` inline extension (`src/addons/`, wired into `bootRuntime` after `unown-tools`; subagents get ask+todo but never the advisor), and the first-generation todo tool (`list`/`add`/`toggle`/`clear`) is retired wholesale. **`ask_user_question`** (a port of @juicesharp/rpiv-ask-user-question, MIT): up to 4 structured questions × 2–4 options (reserved labels `Other`/`Type something.`/`Next` rejected at runtime), an auto-appended "Type something." free-text row, per-question + global notes, single-select previews in a monospace box, a submit-review tab — and the call **blocks the turn until the user answers** (presentation tiers: full TUI dialog in place of the editor; an RPC/ACP walker over native dialogs; headless = a `before_agent_start` reconciler strips the tool from the active set both ways, with a typed decline on stray calls — print mode and JSON subagents never advertise a question nobody can answer). **`todo`** (a port of @juicesharp/rpiv-todo, MIT): `create`/`update`/`list`/`get`/`delete`/`clear` over a 4-state machine (`pending` → `in_progress` → `completed`, plus deleted tombstones; `completed` only → `deleted`), `blockedBy` dependencies with self-block rejection, live-id validation and DFS cycle rejection on the merged graph, `activeForm` progress labels, `owner`/`metadata` fields, a live panel above the editor (row-budget layout with a `+N more` summary; completed rows fade at the next agent turn boundary; model-authored text sanitized of CSI/OSC/bidi controls before rendering), `/todos`, and state riding `unown-todo-state` session entries — branch-correct across `/fork`/`/tree`/`/resume`/compaction — with the `/goal` completion gate reading the same list through `foregroundTodosForGoalGate()`. **The advisor** (a reverse-engineered port of oh-my-pi's advisor/watchdog, MIT) ships **OFF by default** (a second model roughly doubles token traffic per reviewed turn) and foreground-only: an in-process loop over `modelRegistry.complete` watching compact per-turn deltas (full user/assistant text, one-line tool cards, bounded tool IO), verifying with read-only `read`/`grep`/`find`, and advising through the emission guard — empty → noise → rank-aware dedupe (strictly-higher-rank re-raises admitted, blockers exempt) → per-update non-blocker budget with rank displacement — after which nits queue as asides at the next turn boundary (one merged message; also flushed at agent end) while concerns/blockers steer immediately (routing alone, never merging deferred asides); `immuneTurns` after a steer degrade concerns to asides, blockers never degrade; oldest-first context trim at 60% of the model window; self-halt after 3 consecutive failed reviews; `WATCHDOG.md`/`.unown/WATCHDOG.md` and per-user `instructions` append advisor-only system-prompt guidance; delivery surfaces are `<advisory>` XML to the agent plus a transcript card and footer status to the user; `/advisor status|on|off`. One config file `$UNOWN_HOME/agent/addons.json` (seeded only when absent, never clobbering user edits; unknown keys dropped, malformed values degrade to defaults, atomic tmp+rename writes; the only hot-reloaded key is `todo.maxWidgetLines`, re-read per render — everything else binds at registration, `/reload` applies), edited live by the `/addons` TUI configurator (every change persists immediately with its applies-when stated) and by `/advisor on|off`. Counts: unown session tools thirteen → **fourteen** (headless sessions advertise thirteen — ask_user_question is UI-gated); slash commands twenty-five → **twenty-seven** with the advisor on (twenty-six by default — `/advisor` rides the off-by-default addon), plus the split-view display mode's own eleven. Docs: new `docs/addons.md` (the full reference — schema, configurator, keybinding grammar, hot-reload semantics, design contracts) with README, usage, configuration, custom-commands, BEHAVIOR, the docs AGENTS.md ownership list and the self-learning AGENTS.md aligned, and the `cli.ts` HELP session-tools block extended to carry `ask_user_question` + `/addons` + `/advisor` (pinned by the help drift-guard test, now 16 commands). Pinned by `test/addons/` — `config.test.ts` (33: config parsing, key-spec grammar, guidance sanitization, plus the questionnaire state machine, key routing and response envelope), `todo-core.test.ts` (22: transitions, cycle detection, overlay layout, replay), `advisor-core.test.ts` (20: emission guard, delivery policy, delta renderer, trim, watchdog), `advisor-runtime.test.ts` (7: steer/aside channels, noise suppression, immune-turn degradation, agent-end boundary) — with the goal and automations wiring tests updated; full suite 2339 pass / 0 fail / 38 skip. Standing invariant preserved: no fork of the pi substrate — everything lands on documented seams (`registerTool`, `registerCommand`, `registerShortcut`, `ctx.ui.*`, `appendEntry`, `sendMessage`).

### Added

- **bridge: upstream failover — BRIDGE_FALLBACK_* routes requests to a second OpenAI-compatible endpoint (Agnes AI) when the primary cannot serve** — when the primary upstream fails a request with a failover-eligible error and a fallback is configured, the bridge retries it once against the fallback and the client sees a normal 200 (or a verbatim SSE stream) instead of the error. Eligible classes (`isFailoverError` in pure.ts): HTTP 429 — Dashscope's quota throttle ships exactly as 429, measured live 2026-10-20 ("hour allocated quota exceeded" on all three known models — so a throttled coding plan no longer takes the stack down for the rest of the hour) — plus 402/408, 401/403 (a rejected or dead primary key IS "primary not working"; the fallback carries its own provider and key), any 5xx, and the network class including Bun's "Unable to connect" connection-refused phrasing, plus quota/arrears/billing/balance/rate-limit message wording (providers that ship quota errors as plain 400s). Plain 400/404/409/422 schema/model errors do NOT fail over — the same request would fail on the fallback too. The cut-in is immediate: a failover-eligible primary error short-circuits out of `callUpstream` with `failoverCandidate: true` without first burning the primary's 6-attempt / ~90 s retry backoff, the request is rewritten onto `BRIDGE_FALLBACK_MODEL` (empty = keep the routed id, for fallbacks serving the same catalog) and re-dispatched with the fallback's own key. The fallback is a remote endpoint, so it joins the `BRIDGE_MIN_SPACING_MS` serialized spacing throttle and the `BRIDGE_UPSTREAM_TIMEOUT_MS` headers timeout like the primary, and gets normal retry semantics — its own failures surface honestly; there is no third-level failover. Distinct from the existing local lane: `BRIDGE_LOCAL_*` is ROUTING (specific model ids deliberately go to a second upstream; local-lane requests never fail over), `BRIDGE_FALLBACK_*` is FAILOVER (any eligible primary failure transparently retries on a second endpoint) — different mechanisms, both configurable at once. Observability: `GET /health` gains a `fallback {ok, status, url, model}` lane (probed when configured, 4 s timeout) and a `failovers` counter of failovers since process start; `GET /v1/log` and the persistent usage log mark failover-served requests with an `fb:` prefix on the upstream model (e.g. `fb:agnes-3.0-flash`), mirroring the `local:` convention; each failover logs a console line with the primary error and the fallback target. Pricing stays honest: `agnes-3.0-flash` is not in the usage price table, so cost shows as null unless `UNOWN_PRICE_AGNES_3_0_FLASH_IN/OUT` is set. 27 new tests: +18 in pure.test.ts (isFailoverError classification including the live Dashscope 429 shape and Bun's "Unable to connect" phrasing, loadConfig fallback parsing, buildUpstreamBody fallback shaping) and a new hermetic serving-layer suite failover.test.ts (9 tests: spawns the real index.ts on an ephemeral port against two mock Bun.serve upstreams — 429 failover with model rewrite + key swap, plain-400 no-failover, 401 failover, connection-refused failover, SSE streaming failover, fallback-also-down error surfacing, no-fallback backward-compat, the /health lane + counter, the `fb:` log prefix); full bridge suite now 181 tests (176 pass + 5 env-gated VLM skips). Live-verified 2026-10-20: with Dashscope quota-throttled (429 on all three models), the bridge served real streaming agent traffic via agnes-3.0-flash — 4 requests in the first minutes after restart, finish_reason tool_calls, zero client errors.

- **engine: the web results store — a persistent, shared cache behind web_search + webfetch + fetch_url (TTL freshness, ETag revalidation, stale-if-error)** — every unown surface that touches the web (terminal sessions, parallel subagents, engine sandbox runs, automations) is its own process, and until now each had only process-local 10-minute fetch memories: identical queries re-burned provider quotas (Tavily/Brave/Serper credits, IP rate limits on the keyless chain) and re-paid latency for pages that had not changed. The new store (`webcache.ts`) gives all of them one shared, on-disk answer at `~/.unown/cache/web/`: **exact-key only** (the repo's own H20 benchmark refuted near-duplicate semantic serving — outcome-flipping edits embed at cosine 0.95+, so there is deliberately no embedding tier), content-addressed immutable entries on the npm/cacache model (sha256 keys → `entries/<xx>/<hash>.json`, atomic writes, checksum-verified reads, corrupt/unknown-schema self-heal, no read locks, no new dependencies — pure `node:fs`/`node:crypto`, no child processes, no platform branches). Freshness is two-layered, exactly the brief's "not too old / no version change": a **TTL window** (search results 60 min; fetched pages honor the origin's Cache-Control/Expires — heuristic 30 min, capped 24 h — and `no-store`/`no-cache` origins are never stored) plus **RFC 9111 revalidation** — direct fetches capture ETag/Last-Modified and stale entries are replayed as conditional GETs where a 304 means the page did not change and the stored body re-serves without paying for a full response (verified live: bun.sh revalidates over a real ETag round-trip). When every upstream attempt fails (quota exhausted, network down, provider 5xx) the last good answer still serves inside a bounded **stale-if-error** window (24 h search / 7 d fetch) — always flagged. Every answer carries its provenance in the tool result — `· cached (12m old)`, `· revalidated (unchanged, 2h old)`, `· STALE — served after provider error (3h old)` — and cached bytes pass through the same injection-shield choke points as live ones. Parallel identical calls coalesce in-process (four subagents searching the same thing = one provider call); bodies are stored at the 200k ceiling and sliced per `max_chars`, so one entry serves every cap; the store is byte-capped (256 MiB default) with LRU eviction (mtime signal, utimes touch — no hot-path rewrites, amortized sweep + on-demand `unown webcache sweep`); an append-only `events.jsonl` feed (rotated at 10k events) drives all observability. Surfaces: `unown webcache stats [--json] | clear | sweep`, a `unown doctor` line (counts-and-paths only in JSON mode — query text never enters a support bundle), engine endpoints `GET /api/webcache` + `POST /api/webcache/clear|sweep` (clear emits `webcache:cleared` so every open dashboard refetches), and the dashboard's eleventh tab **Web Cache** — KPIs (hit rate, requests saved, cached size vs cap, entries), the store-config card, a requests-saved trend, the color-coded activity feed (hit emerald / revalidated violet / miss zinc / stale amber / evict orange / clear violet / bypass+error rose), per-provider traffic, and confirm-gated Clear + Sweep. Knobs (all optional, `||` convention): `UNOWN_WEB_CACHE=0` kill switch, `UNOWN_WEB_CACHE_DIR`, `UNOWN_WEB_SEARCH_CACHE_TTL_MS`, `UNOWN_WEBFETCH_CACHE_TTL_MS`, `UNOWN_WEB_SEARCH_STALE_MS`, `UNOWN_WEBFETCH_STALE_MS`, `UNOWN_WEB_CACHE_MAX_BYTES`. Design record with the prior-art research (GPTCache skip/borrow, LangChain key-hashing borrow, npm/cacache + RFC 9111 adopt, Claude Code's 15-min WebFetch TTL precedent improved upon): `docs/web-results-store.md`. Pinned by `test/webcache.test.ts` (45: keys/normalization, policy windows, integrity self-heal, disabled mode, eviction invariants, event aggregation, origin TTL parsing, single-flight, env semantics — including the regression guard for the `Number("") === 0` trap the `||` convention exists to prevent), 11 integration tests in `test/websearch.test.ts` (zero-upstream-call assertions on the DashScope zero-hit pattern, 304/200 revalidation, no-store, stale windows incl. an aged-beyond-every-window entry, raw mode, coalescing), `test/cli/webcache-cli.test.ts` + `test/server-webcache.test.ts` (19), and 29 dashboard tests incl. the tab's happy/empty/error trio. Real-network smoke: search 1201 ms live → 1 ms store hit; page fetch revalidated via a genuine origin ETag 304. Visual verification: desktop full-page, mobile 390 px, clear-confirm dialog, tab-click — all SHIP.

- **landing: the capability dex — the agent's ten capabilities as an interactive creature catalog, powered by fuse.js** — the Pokédex milestone (2026-10-20) opens with the research round the brief demanded, and the result is a negative with one honest dependency: the npm/GitHub ecosystem holds **no reusable interactive-Pokédex UI component**. Everything called "pokedex" is a PokéAPI data client (`pokedex-promise-v2`, `pokenode-ts`, `pokeapi-js-wrapper`, `@sherwinski/pokeapi-ts`) or a tutorial/demo app (matheusmhq/pokedex-react-js, mauricius/simple-react-pokedex, IdoBouskila/Pokedex) — and all of them ship official Nintendo artwork and species data, which pokemon.com's legal page (fan art grants no license to Pokémon IP beyond personal noncommercial home use) and this repo's non-affiliation wall both forbid. So the FRLG/Gen-3 two-pane pattern — a scrollable numbered list with a search box, and a detail pane with the specimen, its entry text, category, type badges and its cry — is built in the page's own design system (the same call the eight-band redesign made for tabs: hand-rolled, WAI-ARIA, in the GBA furniture), and the one real dependency is **fuse.js** (npm `fuse.js`, zero-dependency TypeScript fuzzy search, ~7 kB gz; krisk/Fuse, fusejs.io), which drives the interactive search over each entry's name, category, type and entry text — a tool the agent drives, not a decorative install. All ten feature creatures move to one canonical home: new band #3, `id="dex"`, `data-hue="magma"` (the Gen-3 red the actual Pokédex device is — magma is the page's only red section hue; proof keeps it and showcase/gold keeps the no-two-adjacent rule intact), positioned between loop and showcase so the page's argument reads claim → how it learns → what it can do (the catalog) → what it looks like → receipts → identity → record. The hero's session-party strip and the loop bento's tile badges retire in the same change, and the capability copy moves with them — the bento's mechanism descriptions and the hero cli-expander's tool prose live in the dex entries, moved rather than duplicated per the one-fact-one-home rule. Accessibility contract: the entry list is an ARIA listbox with roving tabindex (arrows/Home/End, selection follows focus like the FRLG cursor), the detail pane is `aria-live="polite"`, the search is a labelled input with an N-of-ten counter; every entry carries its receipt link(s) (the source file on GitHub) so the dex keeps the every-number-checkable contract. Each creature gets an original synthesized cry via the Web Audio API (deterministic square-wave chiptune motif per creature — no sampled audio, nothing Nintendo-derived, plays only on explicit click). Scroll budget stays binding: from the measured baseline 12.5vh / 2,603 words / 8 bands the new band is paid for before it is built — removing the hero party strip (−0.1vh), absorbing the loop bento (−0.7vh, its copy moves into the entries), and deleting the alphabet's static DexCard (−0.4vh; it mostly restated hero-seal facts № 0201 / アンノーン / Symbol · Psychic) — and the compensation over-paid: the nine-band page measures 12.0vh / 2,492 words, half a viewport SHORTER than the eight-band page it replaces, every band under the 3vh cap (the dex itself: 1.03vh). Pinned by `__tests__/dex.test.tsx` (7 tests: the listbox contract + roving tabindex, the fuse filtering with counter and esc-to-clear, arrow-key walking with the detail pane following, labelled cry controls that stay inert without an AudioContext, the Parity entry's M1-M4 capability names + all nine receipt links, the generated job sentence, and every entry's name/number in its pane), a rewritten `__tests__/creature.test.tsx` (the dex mounts all ten sprites at 3× logical — h-48, an integer multiple so the pixel grid never resamples — decorative beside labels, and pins that NO creature renders outside the dex: the hero party strip and bento badges are gone, not duplicated), the updated nine-band composition walk in `landing-page.test.tsx`, a moved-not-duplicated assertion in `landing-copy.test.tsx`, and the hero's session-surface test now pinning the #dex pointer. Verified: typecheck, eslint, 325 unit tests, GBA palette gate, contrast audit 1,022 nodes AA in both schemes, production-build screenshots both schemes + mobile + reduced motion, e2e axe (WCAG 2.0/2.1 A+AA, both motion passes) + the scroll-budget spec, and three Agnes VLM review rounds whose verdicts oscillated FIX↔PASS on unchanged elements (the over-trigger the asset workflow documents for chibi aesthetics) — the two structural findings that survived triage were taken (the specimen bumped to 3×, the list shows all ten rows on desktop instead of scrolling rows 9-10 out of sight), and the rest were overruled by the binding gates. Research, the IP reasoning and the negative result documented in `docs/landing-research.md` Part 15.

- **docs: the agentic asset workflow — the creature pipeline generalized into a reusable runbook** — `docs/agentic-asset-workflow.md` captures the process that shipped the feature creatures as a phase-by-phase workflow for any future visual-asset batch (sprites, illustration families, icon sets): pin a checkable acceptance criterion before generating; research style + tooling with subagents (the citations are the IP defense); split labor so the model draws composition while a deterministic pipeline owns every pixel and the palette; hard gates in code (cut sanity, edge-clip on the raw, dark-ratio) with VLM passes as reviewer's aids behind a signifier-audit arbitration protocol; element-level crop reviews because a 64px asset is invisible in a full-page screenshot; the full page verification set in the same change; milestone pushes. It ships with the run's failure catalogue — ten symptom → cause → fix rows (the Pikachu attractor, the lanczos-smoothed review sheet, the 20-ramp-step "airbrushed" verdicts, the zombie next-server "missing chunks" ghost, and the rest) — so none of it has to be re-learned. The landing AGENTS.md points at it from the creature contract.

- **landing: the feature creatures — the agent's features denoted by original GBA-style pixel sprites, generated through a gated pipeline** — the landing page's features (MCP, web search, subagents, LSP, memory, harness, routing, AutoDream, hooks, SDLC parity) are now denoted by 2D "monster companion" sprites in the retro-handheld style the page already speaks. Ten original renders (never Pokémon assets — the non-affiliation wall holds; prompts use generic creature-collector vocabulary and a signifier audit arbitrates every VLM IP flag). The pipeline (`scripts/creature_sprites.mjs`) owns the style so ten sprites match each other and the page: stage 1 generates composition with a locked template (one CONCEPT line per feature, one prop each — a 64px sprite stops reading past one idea); stage 2 GBA-ifies deterministically — box-average to a 64px logical grid, 1-bit alpha (GBA OBJ color 0 is transparent), OKLab snap onto **the landing palette's own tokens** (a sprite inherits the page's world the way a glyph inherits currentColor), prune to 8 colors (Gen-3 two-tone shading), ship 4× nearest at 1.4–1.8 KB each, display 1:1 logical via a `.creature` class (`image-rendering: pixelated`). Hard gates that fail the run: background-cut sanity, edge-clip on the raw (a limb cut at the generator's frame reads broken at any size), and dark-ratio ≤30% (a dark-bodied sprite is a smudge on the light cream scheme — the round's one measured scheme failure, fixed by bright-body steers). Placement: the hero's "session party" strip (four chips labeled web_search/mcp/task/lsp between the seal and the session replay; the cli expander's prose stays canonical) and one creature anchored beside each loop-bento tile's number+title (a floated-top-row placement and a two-sprite pair were both tried and rejected on review; the proof band stays creature-free per the benchmark-costume rule). Every creature is decorative beside its text label (alt="" + aria-hidden, WAI decorative-image rule; the `label` prop covers a future sole-content use). Pinned by `__tests__/creature.test.tsx` (5 tests: every id ships a file, decorative-by-default render incl. pixelated class + intrinsic dims, labelled render, the party chips label every creature, the bento badges all six tiles one creature each) and quickstart.test/landing-copy.test are untouched-green. Verified: typecheck, eslint, 317 unit tests, GBA palette gate, scroll budget 12.5vh/2,575w (from 12.3vh/2,540w — the party strip costs 0.2vh), contrast audit 1,036 nodes both schemes AA, production screenshots both schemes + reduced motion, OG images regenerated, Agnes VLM element-crop review (dark: "looks like native ROM assets"). Research, IP-audit method and the placement verdicts documented in `docs/landing-research.md` Part 14; the creature contract in the landing AGENTS.md. Drive-by: quickstart.test now mocks the StarCount module like landing-copy.test does — its live api.github.com fetch is rate-limited from shared IPs (60/hr unauthenticated) and suspended the Hero past synchronous assertions, failing 4 tests deterministically in this sandbox and flakily anywhere else.

- **release: per-OS standalone `unown` binaries, cross-compiled by one runner and smoke-tested on their own OS** — every `v*.*.*` release now ships six CLI artifacts (`scripts/build-artifacts.ts` → `unown-{darwin,linux,windows}-{arm64,x64}.tar.gz|.zip`) next to the verified tag. `bun build --compile --target=` downloads the target platform's runtime, so a single ubuntu job produces all six and no Mac or Windows machine is needed to build them; the release workflow's `smoke-artifacts` leg then extracts each platform's archive **on that platform** and runs it (five of six legs — GitHub hosts no arm64 Windows runner, so `windows-arm64` ships labelled untested rather than claimed verified). Building the artifacts surfaced three defects the checkout path could not reveal, all fixed here: `bin/unown` has no file extension, so compiling it directly bundled 6 modules and produced a **silent no-op executable** (exit 0, zero output) — the entry is now `src/boot.ts`, which `bin/unown` also calls, so there is still exactly one way unown starts; `store.ts` resolved its repo root through `import.meta.dir` and called `mkdirSync` at module-evaluation time, so a compiled binary died with `EPERM: mkdir '\'` before printing a line — new `src/packaging.ts` is the single module that knows the two runtime shapes (its `$bunfs/root` / `B:\~BUN\root` / `%7EBUN` detections were each measured by running a probe binary on the OS that emits it, and the Windows `~BUN` spelling is the case the previous guard in `tools.ts` missed); and a version read from the checkout's root `package.json` prints `unknown` in a binary, which would have made the release's own version-consistency assertion unsatisfiable — the builder now injects it with `--define __UNOWN_VERSION__`. The binaries are UNSIGNED by decision (no Apple Developer account, no code-signing secret in this repo), so each archive carries an `INSTALL.txt` with the Gatekeeper/SmartScreen step. `bun run release:artifacts` builds them; `bun run test:artifacts` pins the plan (measured failing on injected drift: inverting the x64-only `-baseline` rule turns two tests red). A Windows host cannot produce POSIX-executable archives — NTFS has no mode bits — which is why the release builds on Linux.

- **tui: the split-view display mode — a built-in port of pi-split-view 1.1.0** — every session now carries three optional display surfaces composed as one layout layer over pi's native transcript and editor: a top band with a live Thinking panel and a Tool Usage panel (every call with a status mark and arguments; compact or full detail with an output preview), a right files dock (read/written/seen files newest-first with an edit-diff subpanel; 34–48 columns, hidden below 90), and a response-only transcript mode (user + assistant text, pi's own message components, editor dock untouched). Eleven slash commands (`/split`, `/split-thinking`, `/split-tools`, `/split-tools-view`, `/split-tools-width`, `/files`, `/split-diff`, `/split-trim-response`, `/split-divider`, `/split-height`, `/split-save`) plus a `--no-split` startup flag (routed through the CLI parser: `unown --no-split` starts bare, ignoring saved defaults). Startup defaults persist via `~/.unown/agent/ui-panels.json` — a template, not live state: toggles are session-local so concurrent windows never clobber each other, and only `/split-save` writes it. History (thinking blocks, tool calls, files, the last edit diff) is rebuilt from the session branch on resume and branch switches; buffers are bounded (60 tools / 500 files / 400 thinking lines / 32 KB per stored tool output). Verified live end to end against the Dashscope bridge (kimi-k2.5 + qwen3.7-plus). Docs: [docs/split-view.md](docs/split-view.md).

### Changed

- **landing: the scroll budget re-balanced — the addons strip pays for itself as a disclosure, and the showcase band pays for the strip's remaining height** — the TUI-addons strip landed at 13.3vh against the 13vh gate (the strip grew the page without paying, a breach of the budget contract this fixes). The strip is now a native `<details>` disclosure — the contract's sanctioned move: the summary row keeps the label and the derived `21 user-tunable keys` figure on screen, the body keeps every tile, pitch and receipt, and the whole strip stays in the server-rendered DOM for crawlers and gates alike. The showcase band's own padding absorbs the remainder (py-16/20 → py-12/16 — the band that grew pays) and the changelog band normalizes to the page-standard padding it had drifted from (py-20/24 → py-16/20). Measured: **12.99vh** (from 13.31), every band under the 3vh cap, budget e2e spec green. Drive-by robustness: the two landing axe scans get a 60s timeout (they measure ~31s on slower runners — the 30s default made the gate a coin flip about clock speed, not violations). The interactive-fleet's landing presence is the dex Task entry (entry copy + practice + fleet receipts) plus the changelog rail; its audited captures ship at `public/landing/tui/fleet/` (5 stills + mp4/webm/gif). Verified: 355 app tests, typecheck, eslint, claims gate (62 literals), budget spec, both landing axe passes, contrast audit 1,144 nodes AA in both schemes, production screenshots both schemes + reduced motion + the disclosure's collapsed and expanded states.

### Changed

- **tui: fullscreen is the default interface layout for new installs** — the first-run seed of `~/.unown/agent/settings.json` now pins `"tuiMode": "fullscreen"` (the alt-screen layout engine the split-view band and files dock are composed through). Existing installs keep their saved settings untouched; switch any time with `/settings` (the "TUI mode" entry) or by editing the file.

- **tui: the vendored pi-tui scrollbar engine ported to the 1.3.0 design** — a visible scrollbar now paints its whole column (│ track, ┃ idle / █ active thumb, per-role styled) instead of recoloring single glyphs: `ScrollView` gains `scrollbarTrackStyle` / `scrollbarThumbStyle` (the legacy `scrollbarStyle` stays as a combined default), `isScrollbarActive` and repaint-on-activate; new theme keys `scrollbarTrack` (falls back to `borderMuted`) and `scrollbarThumb` (falls back to `muted`) are wired through the theme schema and every shipped theme.

### Changed

- **landing: the creature atlas, v3 — the variety round, one body plan per creature** — the authored-pixel atlas (v2, same day) was rejected on review within hours: "sprites look too similar, get creative." The diagnosis was structural, not cosmetic — a new variety probe (a VLM pass over the contact sheet asking only how many body plans the set shares) measured 9 of 10 sprites on ONE composition pattern (the standing chibi), variety 3/10, "reskins of the same base character." Silhouette reads first, prop second, palette third; v2 varied the last two and left the first untouched. v3 gives each feature its own body plan: websearch the CRAWLER (a round two-part spider, six legs, the magnifier held high), mcp the SOCKET GOLEM (angular, floating cube head, three gold chest sockets, gold treads — the only hard-cornered creature), task the COIL (a limbless serpent, tail raised, the glowing job balanced on its tip), lsp the BOOKWYRM (an oval head over segments that taper and curve left, round spectacles with cream lenses, an open book held by two nubs), memory the ARCHIVE TURTLE (side view, drawer-shell with gold frames, one card already out), harness the COACH (an upright roo — long ears, stopwatch in the pouch, big feet thrust forward, thick tail), router the SIGNPOST (a courier head wearing two arrow boards with navy chevrons, planted on a pole), dream the SHEEP (scallop-edged wool asleep on a cloud), hooks the GUARD CRAB (eyes up on stalks, smile low on the shell, shield grown across its front), sdlc the WORKS BADGER (a walking quadruped, white blaze, gold vest with navy trim both edges, one badge). Ten plans, ten poses, ten faces — the bestiary now matches by CONSTRUCTION (kit, ramps, gates, baseline 52, uniform 256x256 ship) and differs by DESIGN. Kit additions: `tube(pts, r)` — the capsule primitive (polyline distance) for legs, tails, coils, stalks — plus `both()` region intersection and `face()`'s `cx` parameter (the shared face template travels to off-center heads; mirror math generalizes without changing the default behavior). The dark-ratio gate taught two laws now written into the doctrine and the AGENTS.md contract: the generated outline taxes every separate part 35-45% of its pixels (the first signpost draft measured 46% dark — MASS BEFORE PARTS: one dominant body, stubby appendages; details live INTERIOR where they cost no outline, attached parts pay half, and only the one story-prop may detach), and a ramp's shadow step can be a dark key — B1 was lightened to #d59c5a (lsp's three islands cannot afford dark bands) and the magma creatures shade with 1px bands. The Z-stamps were redrawn with two-pixel stepping diagonals after the orphan gate caught a floating one-pixel outline island (O@42,18) — a 1px diagonal stepping by two orphans through the outline pass. Six contact-sheet review rounds plus two display-scale element crop reviews took the set from variety 3/10 to 9/10 with every intended form reading (spider, golem, serpent, bookwyrm+turtle+roo+signpost+sheep+crab+badger all confirmed), IP-safe against explicit Dratini/Snivy/Krabby/Corphish comparisons. Fixes taken across the rounds: the spider's legs moved out of hiding behind its body (the first draft read frog); the bookwyrm got an oval head, taper, curve, a curl, contrast-corrected glasses (gold-on-amber rings were a gold mask — dark-gold G1 rings with cream lenses) and the open book; the roo went upright with haunches and a navy-bezel stopwatch with a bright face and gold crown (a cream-faced watch on a cream pouch read as one blob — the crop review's one surviving finding); the signpost's arrows grew from the head with cream fields and navy chevrons; memory's drawers got gold frames and wide handles; sdlc's vest got navy trim on both edges and a rosette badge. Overruled with documented mechanisms: lsp's round-5/6 flags flipped PASS-then-FAIL on unchanged pixels with a different rationale each time while the display-scale crop confirmed all three signifiers (2-of-3 by the repeat rule); "harness and sdlc are both quadrupeds" (the roo is an upright biped); "dream's wool dithering is noisy" (the per-bump light arcs are what make wool read as wool). The dex entry copy moved with the forms — websearch "the crawler form", mcp "the many-socketed form", router "the wayfinding form", and rewritten entries for task/lsp/memory/harness/dream/hooks that tell the new body plans (all game-voice, original words; every number and receipt unchanged; word count within budget). Verified: all ten sprites green on every hard gate (dark ratios 19-30%), typecheck, eslint, landing test suite, palette/claims/docs/changelog gates, scroll budget unchanged. Research, the probe instrument, the dark-ratio curriculum and the arbitration receipts: `docs/landing-research.md` Part 17; the workflow gains the set-variety audit as a standing step: `docs/agentic-asset-workflow.md`.
- **landing: the creature atlas — the sprites redrawn as authored pixel art** — the feature creatures shipped in the morning and were rejected on review the same day (2026-10-20): the first set (AI-generated illustrations, box-averaged to 64px and snapped to the page's 21 UI tokens) read as mush — soft outlines on harness/sdlc/hooks, props dissolving into indistinguishable blobs (wrenches, gears, a clock), consistency outliers between sprites, and two IP near-misses (websearch read Cyndaquilt-like, task Growlithe-like). The autopsy found three root causes, none fixable by a better prompt: an AI illustration is smooth art and averaging smooth art onto a pixel grid makes mush; the page's text palette carries one step per hue (an aqua body snaps to #62acee and stops — its only darker blues are desaturated navies), so the snap made flat or muddy shading; and ten independent generations agree only by luck, not construction. The v2 rewrite authors every sprite IN CODE, pixel by pixel, in three files: `scripts/creature_kit.mjs` (the drawing kit — the atlas palette, region predicates composed with union/minus, `rim()` directional cel bands under one top-left light, the outline pass, the shared face template, feet/arms, the `chibi()` standing-creature composer, hand-drawn stamps), `scripts/creature_atlas.mjs` (the ten compositions, ids unchanged, ONE prop each — magnifier + dish ears / dock with 3 gold ports / one psychic orb overhead / monocle + chain / filing drawer with a card slipping out / gold stopwatch / clock belly + courier cap / cloud + star + z's / shield with gold boss / badger blaze + vest + badge), and `scripts/creature_sprites.mjs` rewritten as the pure pipeline (draw → outline pass → deOrphan cleanup → hard gates → render → labeled contact sheet → optional `--review`); the AI-generation lane is removed (git history preserves it). The palette philosophy changed with it: per-family hue-shifted ramps (shadows toward blue/violet, lights toward yellow/white — the pret/pokeemerald palettes do the same), every value on the GBA 5-bit channel grid, hues taken from the page's own families — the page-world link is now inheritance by hue family, and "every sprite colour is a legal GBA colour" is true pixel-for-pixel AND gated (`gatePalette` fails the run on any off-grid value; it caught 23 hand-computed off-grid values during development, which is the gate working). The construction system is measured against 16 Emerald front sprites parsed from pret/pokeemerald (content 23–61px × 22–53px, avg ~41×39; margins avg ~12px all sides; 7–15 visible colors, avg 12.2; shared outline 16,16,16 — near-black, never #000): 64×64 canvas, standing sprites share baseline row 52, head ≈ half the sprite, low-set 3–4px eyes, stubby feet, ≤ 15 visible colors (GBA OBJ: 16 slots, slot 0 transparent; ours use 9–12), one prop per creature — v1 died of two to four. Hard gates, all failing the run: the 5-bit grid (on the palette itself), ≤ 15 colors per sprite, **edge-outline integrity** (every opaque pixel 4-adjacent to transparency must be the outline key — the crispness invariant no downscale can ever satisfy, now true by construction), the 8-neighbor orphan rule with a deterministic deOrphan merge pass (diagonal staircases legitimate, isolated pixels noise; white glints exempt), dark-ratio ≤ 30%, content margins (nothing within 3px of the frame), the standing baseline 52±2, and X-mask symmetry where the composition claims it. The dark-ratio gate drove real design decisions: memory's drawer got a cream face, hooks' shield field went cream with a gold border, sdlc's vest went gold with navy trim, router's clock belly got a thin navy ring, and mcp's three floating sockets became one dock plate with a cream face and three gold ports. Every sprite now ships on the FULL 64px canvas — a uniform 256×256 at 4× nearest, so uniform margins + the shared baseline carry the specimen-sheet consistency (v1 shipped ragged trimmed sizes like 228×256 and 184×256); `creature.tsx`'s DIMS table is uniform for all ten and `__tests__/creature.test.tsx` pins 256/256; display classes unchanged (h-16 = 1:1, the dex detail's h-48 = 3×, `image-rendering: pixelated`). Six Agnes VLM review rounds, the same reviewer's-aid-not-gate protocol: round 1 PASS with craft findings, rounds 2–5 FIX with oscillating flags, round 6 PASS clean (GBA-style with no outliers, IP-safe — "original, abstracted dev-tool mascots" — all props readable, one cohesive family). Taken under the repeat-rule + mechanism triage: task got face room and a belly patch; dream got a chunky roundRect cloud, bigger z's and bigger closed-eye arcs; sdlc's mask band (flagged in four of five rounds) was dropped for a white badger blaze between standard eyes; harness's stopwatch got bigger and closer; websearch's magnifier lens went cream with an aqua glint; lsp's monocle ring went 2px. Overruled with reasons: round 3's "lsp is a Jigglypuff copy" fell to a signifier audit (Jigglypuff = pink balloon + huge blue-green eyes + forehead curl + microphone; lsp carries zero of the four — amber body, small navy eyes, a monocle — and two prior rounds had cleared it), and round 4's "dream has anti-aliased edges" is mechanically impossible (the edge-outline gate proves every boundary pixel is the outline key, and the review sheet is nearest-neighbor resized) — the case for arbitrating against the binding gates; also overruled: "websearch + router cool tones inconsistent" (the page palette is deliberately navy+aqua+gold) and "hooks too bean-shaped" (silhouette variety by design). Verified: typecheck, eslint, 325 unit tests, GBA palette gate (21 dark + 21 light tokens, AA in both schemes), claims gate (51 literals), docs gate, scroll budget UNCHANGED (12.0vh / 2,492 words / 9 bands — the layout did not move, only the PNG bytes and the dims table did), production-build screenshots in both schemes + reduced motion, and dex-band element crops in both schemes. The trigger, the autopsy, the technique research (Slynyrd, Saint11, Lospec, GBATEK, the pret/pokeemerald measurements) and the arbitration stories are documented in `docs/landing-research.md` Part 16.
